Januar 2024 zeitgleich mit dem Gebäudeenergiegesetz/Heizungsgesetz (GEG) in Kraft treten.", "excerpt_sha256": "3a02e1ff47c5bfe16d2052c918a19ad8dc8ab9ab3d25915654868524fe77851b", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0009-E010", "source_id": "SRC-42a395030302baf9", "url": "https://www.bundestag.de/dokumente/textarchiv/2023/kw41-de-waermeplanung-968842", "title": "Bundestag billigt Gesetz zur kommunalen Wärmeplanung", "publisher": "Deutscher Bundestag", "raw_sha256": "2dee65c5f5e728e09cedee42fed0a442346658ef02b19104d9446ab64d01756c", "fact_summary": "On 17 November 2023, the Bundestag passed the municipal heat-planning law, requiring state-level heat planning, 50 percent climate-neutral networked heat by 2030, renewable or unavoidable-waste heat shares of at least 30 percent by 2030 and 80 percent by 2040 in existing networks, and full renewable or waste-heat operation by the end of 2044.", "question_ids": ["5B.1", "5B.6", "6B.1", "8B.2", "8B.6"], "polarity": "favorable", "period_fit": "Enacted in November 2023 with future implementation deadlines", "ruler_attribution": "Scholz coalition government and Bundestag majority", "limitations": ["The law's future outcomes and municipal capacity were contested in the same source."], "start_unit": 40, "end_unit": 43, "locator": "extracted HTML block 40 through extracted HTML block 43", "exact_excerpt": "Weitere Informationen\nAusschuss für Wohnen, Stadtentwicklung, Bauwesen und Kommunen\nAusschussdrucksache 20(24)194 - Formulierungshilfe - Umgang mit der Unionsrechtswidrigkeit von § 13b BauGB\n(Dokument, Link öffnet ein neues Fenster)\nAusschussdrucksache 20(24)195 - Regelungsvorschläge zu den angestrebten Erweiterungen der Außenbereichsprivilegierung von Biomassenlagen\n(Dokument, Link öffnet ein neues Fenster)\nAusschussdrucksache 20(24)196 - Umsetzungsvorschläge zu den angestrebten Regelungen zur Klimaanpassung\n(Dokument, Link öffnet ein neues Fenster)\nHerausgeber\nDeutscher Bundestag, Internetredaktion\nKommunen\nBundestag billigt Gesetz zur kommunalen Wärmeplanung\nDer Bundestag hat am\nFreitag, 17. November 2023\n, den Gesetzentwurf der Bundesregierung für die\nWärmeplanung und zur Dekarbonisierung der Wärmenetze\n(\n20/8654\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n) in der vom Ausschuss für Wohnen, Stadtentwicklung, Bauwesen und Kommunen geänderten Fassung (\n20/9344\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n) angenommen. Dafür stimmten SPD, Bündnis 90/Die Grünen und FDP, dagegen die CDU/CSU, die AfD und die Linksfraktion. Zur Abstimmung lag auch ein Bericht des Haushaltsausschusses gemäß Paragraf 96 der Geschäftsordnung des Bundestages zur Finanzierbarkeit vor (\n20/9369\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n\n).\nEinführung einer flächendeckenden Wärmeplanung\nMit dem Gesetzentwurf werden die rechtlichen Grundlagen für die verbindliche Einführung einer flächendeckenden Wärmeplanung schaffen. Er ergänzt die im September beschlossene Novelle des Gebäudeenergiegesetzes („Heizungsgesetz“) und tritt wie diese am 1. Januar 2024 in Kraft. Ziel ist es, die Versorgung mit Raumwärme, Warmwasser und Prozesswärme auf Treibhausgasneutralität umzustellen, damit die Klimaschutzziele der Bundesregierung bis 2045 erreicht werden können.\nKonkret werden die Bundesländer verpflichtet, auf ihrem Gebiet eine Wärmeplanung durchzuführen. Bis 2030 soll die Hälfte der leitungsgebundenen Wärme klimaneutral erzeugt werden. Den Betreibern bestehender Wärmenetze wird vorgegeben, die Wärmenetze bis 2030 mindestens zu 30 Prozent und bis 2040 zu 80 Prozent mit Wärme zu speisen, die aus erneuerbaren Energien oder unvermeidbarer Abwärme hergestellt wurde. Für neue Wärmenetze wird ein entsprechender Anteil von 65 Prozent verlangt.\nSPD: Koalition beweist Handlungsfähigkeit\nIn der Aussprache sagte\nBernhard Daldrup (SPD),\nmit diesem Gesetz beweise die Koalition ihre Handlungsfähigkeit. Gebraucht werde Tempo und Effizienz in der Klimapolitik, die Bürger erhielten nun Klarheit für ihre Entscheidung zur Wärmeversorgung der eigenen vier Wände.\nDaldrup betonte, dies sei ein „schlankes Gesetz“, das bei den erneuerbaren Energien technologieoffen sei. An die Union gerichtet sagte der SPD-Abgeordnete, man könne nicht Zeitdruck beklagen und mehr Tempo verlangen. „Wir helfen mit Milliardenbeträgen aus dem Klima- und Transformationsfonds“, so Daldrup.\nCDU/CSU: Die Verunsicherung wird bleiben\nDemgegenüber argumentierte der\nCDU-Abgeordnete Dr. Jan-Marco Luczak\n, bei diesem Gesetz handele es sich nicht um „seriöse Arbeit“. Es sei ein strategischer Fehler der Koalition gewesen, das Heizungsgesetz vor der Wärmeplanung zu bringen, diese Reihenfolge sei falsch. Die Wärmeplanung sei zenraler Bezugspuinkt für die Rechtspflichten aus dem Heizungsgesetz.\nGesetzliche Unklarheiten werden aus Sicht Luczaks durch das Gesetz nicht aufgelöst, die Fristen für die Kommunen seien viel zu kurz. Die Verunsicherung bei den Menschen werde bleiben. Es handele sich um ein handwerklich schlechtes Gesetz, so Luczak.\nFDP: Gesetz ist ein Meilenstein\nVon einem „Meilenstein“ sprach hingegen\nDaniel Föst (FDP).\nDas Heizungsgesetz gelte für Bestandsbauten erst nach Vorliegen der Wärmeplanung, sagte er an die Adresse Luczaks. Lediglich für Neubauten gelte es bereits ab 2024.\nMan habe im Gegensatz zur Union Vertrauen in die kommunale Ebene, fügte Föst hinzu. Die Union baue einen Popanz auf und zeige Misstrauen gegenüber den Bürgermeistern.\nGrüne: Planungssicherheit und Verlässlichkeit\nFür\nDr. Julia Verlinden (Bündnis 90/Die Grünen)\nschafft das Gesetz Planungssicherheit und Verlässlichkeit für Industrie, Handwerk und Kommunen. Beim Heizungstausch gebe es eine Förderung von bis zu 70 Prozent. Geschwindigkeit lohne sich auch bei der Gebäudesanierung, da die Zuschüsse für Fenstertausch und die Dämmung von Wänden und Dach erhöht würden.\nMit dem Gesetz werde Planungs- und Investitionssicherheit für die Kommunen geschaffen. Wichtig sei, dass die Klimaverpflichtungen eingehalten und die Kommunen nicht überfordert werden. In großen Städten gebe es bis Sommer 2026 Klarheit über die Wärmeplanung, in kleineren Kommunen bis Sommer 2028, sagte Verlinden. Den Wärmenetzbetreibern werde vorgegeben, erneuerbare Energien in das Netz zu integrieren und Abwärme zu verwenden. „Wir haben das richtige Fundament gelegt“, so die Abgeordnete.\nAfD: Weg in die Knechtschaft\n\nNach Ansicht von\nCarolin Bachmann (AfD)\nhaben die Kommunen weder das Geld noch das Personal für die Wärmeplanung. Mit dem Ziel, bis 2045 klimaneutral zu werden, werde die Koalition scheitern, prognostizierte sie. Ein Ausweg wäre aus ihrer Sicht die Rückkehr zur Kernenergie.\nMit diesem Wärmeplanungsgesetz zeige die Regierung ihr „planwirtschaftliches Antlitz“, betonte Bachmann und sprach vom „Weg in die ökosozialistische Knechtschaft“.\nLinke zweifelt am Erfolg des Gesetzes\nRalph Lenkert (Die Linke)\nnannte es mehr als fraglich, ob dieses Gesetz angesichts der Geldknappheit der Kommunen und des Mangels an Fachpersonal klappt. Ohne Wärmeplanung bleibe für Hausbesitzer und Mieter nicht abschätzbar, welche Heizart für sie künftig sinnvoll ist. Dort, wo Wärmenetze verfügbar seien, müssten diese erste Wahl sein.\nNur in Einfamilienhaussiedlungen am Ortsrand, wo kein Gasnetz liegt und Wärmenetze unrentabel sind, könne man sofort entscheiden und die Förderung inklusive Springer-Bonus beim Einbau einer Wärmepumpe kassieren. Alle anderen müssten unverschuldet warten und erhielten eine geringere Förderung. „Das ist ungerecht“, so Lenkert.\nWärmepläne in Städten und Gemeinden\nErgebnis der Wärmeplanung sind Wärmepläne, die in Städten mit mehr als 100.000 Einwohnern bis Ende Juni 2026 und in kleineren Städten und Gemeinden bis Ende Juni 2028 erstellt werden müssen. Für Gemeinden mit weniger als 10.000 Einwohnern können die Länder ein vereinfachtes Verfahren vorsehen. Auch können mehrere Gemeinden eine gemeinsame Wärmeplanung vornehmen. Bis Ende 2044 muss jedes Wärmenetz vollständig mit Wärme aus erneuerbaren Energien, aus unvermeidbarer Abwärme oder aus einer Kombination daraus gespeist werden.\nZu den vom Bauausschuss vorgenommenen Änderungen zählt die Modifikation und Ergänzung mehrerer Regelungen im Wärmeplanungsgesetz. Unter anderem wird der Anteil der Biomasse an der jährlich erzeugten Wärmemenge in Wärmenetzen von mehr als 50 Kilometern Länge ab 2045 auf maximal 15 Prozent begrenzt. Gestrichen wurde die Vorgabe, dass der Biomasse-Anteil bei einer Netzlänge von 20 bis 50 Kilometern auf 25 Prozent begrenzt werden soll.\nPrivilegierung von Biomasse im Außenbereich\nDer Gesetzentwurf enthält auch Änderungen des Gesetzes über die Umweltverträglichkeitsprüfung und des Baugesetzbuches. So soll die energetische Nutzung von Biomasse im baulichen Außenbereich planungsrechtlich erleichtert werden. Dazu erhält der Paragraf 246d des Baugesetzbuches Sonderregelungen, die bis Ende 2028 befristet sind.\nPrivilegiert werden sollen Vorhaben, die der Aufbereitung von Biogas zu Biomethan dienen oder die mit bestimmten Vorgaben als Blockheizkraftwerk Strom oder Wärme erzeugen. Die Befristung bezieht sich nicht auf die Geltungsdauer einer Genehmigung, sondern auf den Zeitraum, bis zu dessen Ende ein Antrag eingegangen sein muss.\nGesetzesänderung nach Bundesverwaltungsgericht-Urteil\nZum zweiten wird der Paragraf 13b im Baugesetzbuch gestrichen und durch einen neuen Paragrafen 215a ersetzt. Nach Paragraf 13b konnten Außenbereichsflächen unter bestimmten Voraussetzungen im beschleunigten Verfahren ohne Umweltprüfung überplant werden. Das Bundesverwaltungsgericht hatte am 18. Juli 2023 einen solchen Bebauungsplan für unwirksam erklärt und dies mit der Unvereinbarkeit mit EU-Recht begründet.\nParagraf 215a soll es nun ermöglichen, nach 13b begonnene Planverfahren geordnet zu Ende zu führen und abgeschlossene Pläne, die an einem beachtlichen Fehler leiden und unwirksam sind, im ergänzenden Verfahren in Kraft zu setzen. Schließlich sollen Naturerfahrungsräume künftig nicht nur in Bebauungsplänen, sondern bereits in Flächennutzungsplänen festgesetzt werden können.\nEntschließungsanträge der Unionsfraktion abgelehnt\nDer Bundestag lehnte zwei Entschließungsanträge der Unionsfraktion ab. Im ersten (\n20/9350\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n), bei dem sich die AfD-Fraktion enthielt, wurde die Regierung unter anderem aufgefordert, das Heizungsgesetz zurückzunehmen und mit einem „umsetzbaren, technologieoffenen Wärmeplanungsgesetz“ neu vorzulegen. Dabei sollte sie sich an den Interessen und Möglichkeiten der Kommunen und den Besonderheiten ländlicher Räume zu orientieren.\nIm zweiten Entschließungsantrag (\n20/9351\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n\n), den alle anderen Fraktionen ablehnten, wurde verlangt, den Paragrafen 13b unter Beachtung von EU-Recht zu reaktivieren und die schnelle, unbürokratische Aufstellung von Bebauungsplänen am Ortsrand zu ermöglichen.\nAfD-Antrag abgelehnt\nEbenfalls gegen die Stimmen der Antragsteller lehnte der Ausschuss einen Antrag der AfD-Fraktion (\n20/8742\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n) ab, eine „sichere, lückenlose und bezahlbare Energieversorgung zu gewährleisten“. Dazu sei die Wiederinbetriebnahme von Kernkraftwerken sowie eine Belieferung durch Pipeline-Gas, inklusive der Nord-Stream-Pipeline, nötig.\nZudem solle die Pflicht für alle Kommunen, eine Wärmeplanung aufzustellen, aufgehoben werden und „der Anschlusszwang für Fernwärmenetze“ beendet werden. Das Wärmeplanungsgesetz solle im Bundestag nicht weiter vorangetrieben werden. Auch sei auf die Abschaffung des Gebäudeenergiegesetzes hinzuwirken, heißt es in dem Antrag. (vom/17.11.2023)\nReden zu diesem Tagesordnungspunkt\nBildinformationen\nAydan Özoğuz\n© Deutscher Bundestag / Stella von Saldern\nÖzoguz, Aydan\nBundestagsvizepräsidentin\n()\nBildinformationen\nBernhard Daldrup\n© Bernhard Daldrup/ Elias Domsch\nDaldrup, Bernhard\nSPD\n()\nBildinformationen\nJan-Marco Luczak\n© Jan-Marco Luczak/ Tobias Koch\nLuczak, Dr. Jan-Marco\nCDU/CSU", "excerpt_sha256": "e8c8bb21554ece9989392c351e74120908d742c200f0a9c4ce4cb8fb6f405d86", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0009-E011", "source_id": "SRC-60a3eb49fd3d4236", "url": "https://www.bundestag.de/dokumente/textarchiv/2023/kw38-de-onlinezugangsgesetz-965016", "title": "Kritik am Stand der Verwaltungsdigitalisierung", "publisher": "Deutscher Bundestag", "raw_sha256": "adc350cca41a0fac234b5b8bcfd7b40d87c7aa5b8e62639b4942e6365aaf1cb9", "fact_summary": "In September 2023, the federal government proposed an amendment to the Online Access Act to institutionalize federal-state cooperation, provide central citizen-account and mailbox services, introduce qualified electronic seals, and permit digital-only procedures for business services.", "question_ids": ["5B.6", "8B.1", "8B.2", "8B.6"], "polarity": "favorable", "period_fit": "Government proposal introduced in September 2023", "ruler_attribution": "Scholz federal government", "limitations": ["The source records a proposal and referral to committees, not completed 2023 implementation."], "start_unit": 24, "end_unit": 24, "locator": "extracted HTML block 24", "exact_excerpt": "Texte (2021-2026)\n()\n1. Lesung\n1. Lesung (Union)\nAnhörung\n2./3. Lesung\nVermittlungsausschuss\n2./3. Lesung (Vermittlungsausschuss)\nInneres\nRegierung will Onlinezugangsgesetz novellieren\nDer Gesetzentwurf der Bundesregierung „zur\nÄnderung des Onlinezugangsgesetzes\nsowie weiterer Vorschriften zur Digitalisierung der Verwaltung (OZG-Änderungsgesetz – OZGÄndG)“ (\n20/8093\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n) stand am\nMittwoch, 20. September 2023,\nauf der Tagesordnung des Parlaments. Nach der Debatte überwiesen die Abgeordneten die Vorlage an die Ausschüsse. Bei den weiteren Beratungen übernimmt der Ausschuss für Inneres und Heimat die Federführung.\nGesetzentwurf der Bundesregierung\nDurch die Regelung sollen die neu geschaffenen Strukturen der Bund-Länder-Zusammenarbeit verstetigt und eine einfache, moderne und digitale Verfahrensabwicklung im übergreifenden Portalverbund ermöglicht werden. Dafür will der Bund unter anderem zentrale Basisdienste bereitstellen und so landeseigene Entwicklungen für das Bürgerkonto und das Postfach ersetzen. Außerdem soll ein schriftformersetzendes qualifiziertes elektronisches Siegel und eine Regelung zu Digital-Only für Unternehmensleistungen eingeführt werden.\nDer Nutzen des Gesetzentwurfs besteht aus Sicht der Bundesregierung darin, „dass er den Prozess der Entwicklung nutzerfreundlicher digitaler Services weiter fördert“. Dies vereinfache das Leben der Menschen und die Tätigkeit von Unternehmen und Selbständigen, soweit diese staatliche Leistungen in Anspruch nähmen. (joh/hau/20.09.2023)\nReden zu diesem Tagesordnungspunkt\nBildinformationen\nAydan Özoğuz\n© Deutscher Bundestag / Stella von Saldern", "excerpt_sha256": "73a33031c77bfe795a47803235052bf185079a58ef8fa09b202dc253ecc82a6d", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0009-E012", "source_id": "SRC-60a3eb49fd3d4236", "url": "https://www.bundestag.de/dokumente/textarchiv/2023/kw38-de-onlinezugangsgesetz-965016", "title": "Kritik am Stand der Verwaltungsdigitalisierung", "publisher": "Deutscher Bundestag", "raw_sha256": "adc350cca41a0fac234b5b8bcfd7b40d87c7aa5b8e62639b4942e6365aaf1cb9", "fact_summary": "At the October 2023 hearing, experts reported that only 105 of the 575 services targeted for online access by the end of 2022 were online at the previous year-end and agreed that the original Online Access Act had missed its target; they also found the 2023 amendment insufficient, citing absent standards, timelines, cost clarity, independent monitoring, security rules, and a common technical model.", "question_ids": ["5B.6", "5B.7", "8B.5", "8B.6", "8B.8"], "polarity": "adverse", "period_fit": "2023 implementation assessment and hearing", "ruler_attribution": "Independent experts assessing the federal government's digital-administration programme", "limitations": ["The implementation shortfall began under the 2017 law and is not solely attributable to Scholz."], "start_unit": 33, "end_unit": 36, "locator": "extracted HTML block 33 through extracted HTML block 36", "exact_excerpt": "Kritik am Stand der Verwaltungsdigi­talisierung\nZeit:\nMontag, 9. Oktober 2023, 14 Uhr\nOrt:\nBerlin, Paul-Löbe-Haus, Sitzungssaal E 600\nSachverständige aus Wirtschaft, Wissenschaft, Zivilgesellschaft und Kommunalverbänden haben am\nMontag, 9. Oktober 2023,\ndie Forderung bekräftigt, die Digitalisierung der öffentlichen Verwaltung mit Nachdruck voranzutreiben. Dazu sei der vorliegende Entwurf einer Novelle des\nOnlinezugangsgesetzes\n(OZG) (\n20/8093\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n) zwar ein „Schritt in die richtige Richtung“, aber nach wie vor unzureichend, hieß es in einer Anhörung im Ausschuss für Inneres und Heimat.\nMit dem 2017 verabschiedeten Onlinezugangsgesetz hatten sich Bund und Länder die Aufgabe gestellt, den Bürgern bis Ende 2022 ein Paket von 575 staatlichen Dienstleistungen auf digitalem Wege zugänglich zu machen. Zum vergangenen Jahreswechsel waren davon indes nur 105 Angebote online geschaltet, mittlerweile sind es einige mehr. Dass das Gesetz sein Ziel bisher verfehlt hat, war Konsens unter den Sachverständigen. Umstritten war, ob die Einführung eines gesetzlichen Rechtsanspruchs auf digitale Verwaltungsleistungen, wie ihn die Unionsfraktion in einem Antrag (\n20/4313\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n) fordert, ein geeignetes Mittel der Beschleunigung sei.\nStaatliches Handeln im digitalen Zeitalter\nDer ehemalige Bevollmächtigte des Saarlandes für Innovation und Strategie,\nAmmar Alkassar\n, würdigte in der Anhörung das OZG „trotz aller Defizite“ als bisher umfangreichste Initiative, um staatliches Handeln auf das digitale Zeitalter einzustellen. Die jetzt vorliegende Novelle sei „positiv“, bleibe aber „hinter den Erfordernissen einer raschen Verwaltungsdigitalisierung“ zurück, sagte Alkassem. Es fehlten nicht nur „einige grundlegende Bausteine“, sondern ein „gemeinsames Zielbild“ im Sinne eines „plattformbasierten Ökosystems der öffentlichen Hand“. Zudem sei „Überkomplexität“ abzubauen: „Eine konsequente Umsetzung der Vewaltungsdigitalisierung erfordert einen echten Einschnitt“, mahnte Alkassem.\n„Nach fünf Jahren OZG ist Deutschland nicht dort, wo es in der Verwaltungsdigitalisierung sein sollte“, klagte\nGrudrun Aschenbrenner\nvon\nVorstand der Anstalt für kommunale Datenverarbeitung in Bayern\n\n. Sie wünsche sich „disruptive Änderungen“ und mehr Mut. In anderen Ländern gelte längst das Prinzip, Verwaltungsvorgänge nur noch digital abzuwickeln: „Was hält uns davon ab?“\nStandards, Zielsetzung und Kosten\nAls Vertreterin des\nDeutschen Städtetages\nwies\nUda Bastians\nauf die zunehmende Personalnot der öffentlichen Verwaltung bei wachsenden Anforderungen hin, die die Umstellung auf digitale Verfahren zu einem zwingenden Gebot werden ließen. Bis 2030 werde ein Drittel der derzeit Beschäftigten in den Ruhestand gehen. Zugleich drehe sich das Karussell der Gesetzesnovellen immer schneller. So sei allein das Aufenthaltsrecht seit 2015 nicht weniger als 59 Mal geändert worden: „Die Digitalisierung ist der Schlüssel, um das Land am Laufen zu halten.“ Gemessen daran bleibe der vorliegende Entwurf hinter den Erwartungen zurück. Es fehlten klare Festlegungen auf Standards, Zielsetzung und Kosten.\nFür den\nDeutschen Landkreistag\nwies\nAriane Berger\ndie Forderung nach einem Rechtsanspruch auf digitale Verwaltungsleistungen, der nach Vorstellung der Union 2025 in Kraft treten soll, als untauglich zurück. Die kommunale IT müsse für die Erfordernisse einer weitgehenden Digitalisierung „aufnahmefähig“ gemacht werden. Es bedürfe eines „Standardisierungprogramms“ für die Verwaltung.\nRechtsanspruch auf digitale Verwaltungsleistungen\n„Grundsätzlich begrüßenswert“, aber nicht ausreichend, um das „\nE-Government\n-Entwicklungsland“ in einen „digitalen Staat“ zu verwandeln, nannte\nJonas Botta\nvom\nDeutschen Forschungsinstitut für öffentliche Verwaltung in Speyer\nden vorliegenden Gesetzentwurf und empfahl, ihn „nachzuschärfen“. Die Novelle müsse, wie das bisherige OZG, erneut eine Frist für die Umsetzung enthalten. Es bedürfe klarer Kriterien, um den Realisierungsfortschritt durch eine unabhängige Stelle regelmäßig evaluieren zu lassen. Nicht zuletzt erklärte Botta einen Rechtsanspruch auf digitale Verwaltungsleistungen für sinnvoll.\nAuch\nMarc Danneberg\nvon\nBranchenverband Bitcom\nsah hier eine Möglichkeit, um den „Umsetzungsdruck“ zu erhöhen. Das Gesetz müsse „Planungssicherheit“ gewährleisten sowie Nutzern einen „niedrigschwelligen“ und flexiblen Zugang zu digitalen Verwaltungsleistungen ermöglichen. Es müsse auch unterschiedliche Wege eröffnen, um Zahlungen an Behörden zu leisten.\n\n„International einmaliges Gesetz“\nAnnette Guckelberger, Professorin für Öffentliches Recht an der Universität des Saarlandes,\nerklärte es für „vertretbar“, dass der Entwurf im Unterschied zum bisherigen OZG keine „pauschale Umsetzungsfrist“ enthält. Es müsse aber einen Zeitplan geben und jährliche Fortschrittsberichte, die an den Bundestag zu erstatten seien. Einen Rechtsanspruch halte sie nicht für sinnvoll, sagte Guckelberger. Digitalisierung sei ein komplexer Prozess, das OZG darin nur einer von mehreren Schritten. Auch die Länder seien gefragt, Regelungen zu beschließen.\nDas OZG sei gescheitert, stellte\nProfessor Gerhard Hammerschmid\nvon der\nBerliner Hertie-\nSchool\nfest. Es sei in seiner Zeit zwar ein „international einmaliges Gesetz“ gewesen, aber mit einem so hohen Anspruch, dass es „zum Scheitern verurteilt“ gewesen sei. Hammerschmid sprach sich gegen einen Rechtsanspruch aus, der „administrativ nicht umsetzbar“ sei, dafür umso nachdrücklicher für ein „verbindliches und transparentes“ Monitoring durch unabhängige Experten, zumal, da es künftig keine verpflichtende Umsetzungsfrist mehr geben solle.\nKompetenzen zwischen Bund und Ländern\n„Wir müssen uns gemeinsam auf die zentralen Dinge konzentrieren und das umsetzen, was schnell Nutzen bringt“, mahnte\nAlexander Handschuh\nvom\nDeutschen Städte- und Gemeindebund\n. Er wandte sich gegen die Absicht des Gesetzgebers, mit dem vorliegende Entwurf die Kommunen direkt statt allein der Länder für die Digitalisierung in die Pflicht zu nehmen. Dies schaffe „keinen Mehrwert“, ebenso wenig wie die Forderung nach einem Rechtsanspruch.\nInga Karrer\nvon der\nKoordinationsstelle\nE-Governmen\nt bei der Deutschen Industrie- und Handelsammer\ngab zu bedenken, dass eine erfolgreiche Digitalisierungsstrategie möglicherweise bedeute, die Kompetenzen zwischen Bund und Ländern im Grundgesetz neu zu verteilen. Sie erfordere ein Maß an Zentralisierung, das nach aktueller Verfassungslage nicht zulässig sei.\nSicherheit der Nutzerkonten und der Postfächer\nAls „Teil der digitalen Zivilgesellschaft“, deren Leben mit der Gesetzesnovelle „schneller und einfacher“ werden solle, stellte sich\nBianca Kastl\nvom\n\nBerliner Innovationsverbund Öffentliche Gesundheit\nvor. Sie bemängelte fehlende Klarheit und Transparenz im vorliegenden Entwurf. Dieser können „allenfalls ein Auftaktprozess einer zukunftsfähigen Verwaltung sein, die bei den Menschen ankommt, mehr leider nicht“.\n„Klare gesetzliche Vorgaben für die Sicherheit der Nutzerkonten und der Postfächer“ mahnte der Bundesdatenschutzbeauftragte Ulrich Kelber an. Auch er stellte fest, Deutschland sei „gefährlich unterdigitalisiert“. Jörg Kremer als Vertreter der Föderalen IT-Kooperation, einer in Frankfurt ansässigen Anstalt öffentlichen Rechts, die Bund und Länder bei der Verwaltungsdigitalisierung unterstützen soll, nannte das bestehende OZG „suboptimal“, aber doch zugleich eine „Initialzündung“ für digitalen Wandel. Erforderlich sei, die oftmals zu trägen Entscheidungs- und Abstimmungsprozesse in der öffentlichen Verwaltung der digitalen Welt anzupassen. Malte Spitz von Nationalen Normenkontrollrat warnte, dass auch die OZG-Novelle scheitern könnte, wenn sie nicht nachgebessert werde.\nGesetzentwurf der Bundesregierung\nMit dem Gesetzentwurf sollen die neu geschaffenen Strukturen der Bund-Länder-Zusammenarbeit verstetigt und eine einfache, moderne und digitale Verfahrensabwicklung im übergreifenden Portalverbund ermöglicht werden. Dafür will der Bund unter anderem zentrale Basisdienste bereitstellen und so landeseigene Entwicklungen für das Bürgerkonto und das Postfach ersetzen. Außerdem soll ein schriftformersetzendes qualifiziertes elektronisches Siegel und eine Regelung zu\nDigital-Only\nfür Unternehmensleistungen eingeführt werden.\nDer Nutzen des Gesetzentwurfs besteht aus Sicht der Bundesregierung darin, „dass er den Prozess der Entwicklung nutzerfreundlicher digitaler\nServices\nweiter fördert“. Dies vereinfache das Leben der Menschen und die Tätigkeit von Unternehmen und Selbständigen, soweit diese staatliche Leistungen in Anspruch nähmen.\nAntrag der Union\nDie Unionsfraktion fordert eine gesetzliche Nachfolgeregelung, die die Fortsetzung des auslaufenden Onlinezugangsgesetzes (OZG) sichert. In ihrem Antrag heißt es, es brauche einen rechtlichen, zeitlichen und finanzieller Rahmen für ein OZG 2.0, um den Ländern und Kommunen Planungssicherheit zu geben. Der IT-Planungsrat müsse vor Beginn des formalen Gesetzgebungsverfahren in ausreichender Form beteiligt werden, schreibt die Fraktion weiter. Die Bundesregierung solle zudem einen Rechtsanspruch der Bürgerinnen und Bürger ab dem 1. Januar 2025 auf die im OZG definierten Verwaltungsleistungen des Bundes vorlegen.\nIm Hinblick auf den Umsetzungsrückstand zum Fristablauf des OZG soll die Bundesregierung sich dafür einsetzen, dass „einzelne verfügbare Online-Leistungen auch flächendeckend für alle Nutzerinnen und Nutzer beschleunigt umgesetzt werden“, um eine Zersplitterung des Bereitstellungangebotes zu verhindern, fordert die Fraktion. Über eine gezielte Kommunikationsstrategie in Zusammenarbeit mit allen OZG-Dienstleistern müsse zudem der Bekanntheitsgrad und das Nutzungsverhalten von digitalen Verwaltungsdienstleistungen bei den Bürgerinnen und Bürgern unterstützt werden. Number of staff and nature of the first instance authority\n\n     Name in English              Number of staff          Ministry responsible            Is there any political\n                                                                                      interference possible by the\n                                                                                         responsible Minister with\n                                                                                          the decision making in\n                                                                                       individual cases by the first\n                                                                                            instance authority?\n                                 8,447 positions\n     Federal Office for                                     Federal Ministry of\n                                 (7,355 full-time\n      Migration and                                          the Interior and                    Yes     No\n                               positions in various\n     Refugees (BAMF)                                           Community\n                              asylum departments)\n\nSource: Information provided by the BAMF, up to date as of 15 December 2023.\n\nThe BAMF is responsible for examining applications for international protection and competent to take\ndecisions at first instance.\n\nThe BAMF has branch offices in all Federal States. As of February 2024, the BAMF website lists a total\nof 58 branch offices.33 The branch offices process the asylum procedures, but also carry out additional\ntasks (for instance, they function as contact points for authorities and organisations active in the\nintegration of foreign nationals, while some branch offices work exclusively on Dublin cases). Branch\noffices are assigned specific countries of origin, whereas the main countries of origin are processed in the\n\n\n\n33\n        BAMF, Locations, available at: https://bit.ly/3dFTd8w.\n\n\n                                                          23\n\nmajority of branch offices.34 In cooperation with the Federal States, the BAMF manages a distribution\nsystem for asylum seekers known as Initial Distribution of Asylum Seekers (Erstverteilung der\nAsylbegehrenden, EASY) system, which allocates places according to a quota system known as\n“Königsteiner Schlüssel” (see Asylum Act). The quota is based on the size and the economic strength of\nthe Federal States in which the centres are located. Furthermore, the system takes into account which\nbranch office of the BAMF deals with an asylum seeker's country of origin.\n\nAs of November 2023, the BAMF had 7.355 positions or “full-time job equivalents” working on various\naspects of asylum (meaning that the actual number of staff is much higher, since many of these positions\nare shared by people working part-time). Since the office is responsible for several other tasks on top of\nthe asylum procedure (e.g., research, integration, migration for reasons other than asylum and return\npolicies), not all staff members are working in the area of asylum. The overall number of positions is 8,138\naccording to the Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community and Community.35\n\nThe government provided the following numbers for positions in the relevant departments as of November\n2023:36\n        v asylum department (excluding revocation and Dublin procedures): 2,327.9 full-time\n          equivalents;\n        v revocation procedures: 105.2 full-time equivalents;\n        v procedures (appeal procedures, representation of the BAMF in court): 376.0 full-time\n          equivalents;\n        v quality management: 141.0 full-time equivalents;\n        v Dublin-procedures: 338.4 full-time equivalents.\n\nThe BAMF also has special officers for security issues. They are responsible for a whole range of issues\nand should be involved in asylum procedures whenever indications arise for instances of ‘extremism,\nterrorism, criminality, human trafficking, war crimes, crimes against humanity and smuggling of human\nbeings’.37 The special officers for security issues act as contact points between the BAMF and other\nauthorities, but they do not necessarily take part in interviews or take over responsibility of particular\nasylum procedures.\n\nQuality\n\nThe quality of BAMF asylum decisions has been much debated in recent years given the high number of\nappeals filed at the courts, but also because of “scandals” which prompted extensive media coverage in\n2017 and 2018.38 This was related, in part, to the high increase in personnel in 2015 and 2016 – likely\ndue to the spike in asylum applications –, accompanied by shortened training phases, with some decision-\nmakers not having received relevant training. As a result, the BAMF has undertaken several changes to\nthe training provided to decision-makers and to the quality assurance procedures since 2017. As of 2018,\nshort summaries of interview transcripts and notice letters are checked by a second employee. Randomly\nselected cases are subject to a more thorough quality control by the BAMF’s quality assurance division.\nIn addition, the BAMF also has a division for ‘Operative management of asylum procedures and\nintegration’ which ‘analyses developments and trends so that it is possible to recognise and react to a\nneed to act for management at an early date’, according to the BAMF.39 In particular, the decision-making\npractices of the different branch offices are monitored and branch offices with significant deviations from\n\n34\n      A list of all countries of origin and the allocated branch offices is available on the website of the Refugee\n      Council of Lower Saxony (up to date as of March 20221): https://bit.ly/3WJ0eg1.\n35\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community, ‘Bundesamt für Migration und Flüchtlinge’, available in German\n      at: https://bit.ly/3qTH0qt.\n36\n      Federal Government, Response to information request by The Left, 20/9933, 28 December 2023, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/42NJDMa, 30-31.\n37\n      BAMF, Entscheiderbrief (newsletter for decision-makers) 4/2020, 1.\n38\n      For more detailed information, see AIDA, Country Report Germany - Update on the year 2019, July 2020,\n      available at: https://bit.ly/34so09M, 20-21.\n39\n      BAMF, Procedure management and quality assurance, 28 November 2018, available at: http://bit.ly/3DxsTgJ.\n\n\n                                                        24", "excerpt_sha256": "8e89c9fc9799b9e48de2bb9d933ba0310ce8d043d23aba81d6af8ac40819ba38", "verification_status": "corrected", "verification_notes": ["The excerpt does not support the claim that monitoring results were not automatically public."]}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0011-R01-E014", "source_id": "SRC-a007cee33f0d9205", "url": "https://asylumineurope.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/AIDA-DE_2023-Update.pdf", "title": "Country Report: Germany; Border monitoring\n\nThere is no independent border monitoring mechanism in Germany.\n\n        1.2. Legal access to the territory\n\nOn top of family reunification, there are two main ways for asylum seekers to legally access the German\nterritory: via the Government’s resettlement and humanitarian admission programmes and via relocation\nfrom other EU Member States. In addition, a specific admission programme for Afghan nationals was\nintroduced in October 2022 (see Differential treatment of specific nationalities in the procedure).\n\nResettlement\n\nSince 2016, the German resettlement programme is part of Germany’s contribution to the EU resettlement\nscheme.85 Next to the national quota, resettlement includes admissions of Syrian refugees from Türkiye\nin the context of the so-called EU-Turkey statement. In addition, the Federal Government can decide on\nhumanitarian admission programmes on an ad hoc, temporary basis. Such a temporary humanitarian\nadmission programme was in place for 20,000 Syrian refugees between 2013 and 2015.86\n\nIn the resettlement programme, the BAMF is responsible for the selection process together with the\nUNHCR. Once resettled refugees arrive in Germany, they first stay in a reception centre for up to two\nweeks. Whereas in previous years, all resettlement refugees were first housed in the reception centre of\nFriedland (Lower Saxony), the resettlement guidelines for 2022 foresee housing in Friedland as well as\nDoberlug-Kirchhain (Brandenburg) or other facilities made available by Federal States.87 They are then\nallocated to a municipality, where they are issued a residence permit which is equivalent in rights to\nresidence permits granted to recognised refugees.88\n\nIn 2019, the German government introduced an additional private sponsorship programme in the form of\na pilot scheme with 500 additional places. In the programme called “Neustart im Team (NesT)” groups of\nat least four persons commit to accompany and support resettled refugees for at least one year and to\npay for their rent during two years. This was lowered to one year on 1 July 2022.89 The Federal\ngovernment decided to make the programme permanent from 1 January 2023, with 200 places available\n\n\n\n81\n      Information provided by the Federal Police, 14 March 2023.\n82\n      Federal Government, Reply to parliamentary question by The Left, 20/8274, 07 September 2023, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/49GtKZX.\n83\n      Mediendienst Integration, Zahl der Flüchtlinge, January 2024, available in German at: https://bit.ly/48tH9n3.\n84\n      Mediendienst Integration, Ein Jahr humanitäre Krise an der Belarus-EU-Grenze, 5 August 2022, available in\n      German at: http://bit.ly/3JHlvBE.\n85\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community, Resettlement und humanitäre Aufnahmen, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/3H4rqhK.\n86\n      resettlement.de,‘Humanitarian admission programmes’, available at: https://bit.ly/3fSx62o.\n87\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community, Anordnung des Bundesministeriums des Innern und für\n      Heimat für das Resettlement-Verfahren 2022 gemäß § 23 Abs. 4 des Aufenthaltsgesetzes (AufenthG) zur\n      Aufnahme besonders schutzbedürftiger Flüchtlinge unterschiedlicher Staatsangehörigkeit oder staatenloser\n      Flüchtlinge aus Ägypten, Jordanien, Kenia und Libanon sowie über den UNHCR Evakuierungsmechanismus\n      in Niger (aus Libyen) vom 24.03.2022, 24 March 2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3TIdpNv.\n88\n      resettlement.de, ‘Resettlement, available at: https://bit.ly/3qVMD7P.\n89\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community, Pilotprogramm NesT wird reguläres Aufnahmeprogramm für\n      Flüchtlinge, 01 July 2022, available in German at: http://bit.ly/3zapdi5.\n\n\n                                                       30\n\nper year. The conditions were slightly changed: groups of four people can apply to be sponsors; and in\ncontrast to the pilot phase, they only need to pay rent (without electricity, water and heating) for one year.90\n\nThe Federal States also run admission programmes mainly for Syrian nationals, but these are mostly\ngeared towards family members of beneficiaries of international protection residing in the respective\nFederal States (see Family Reunification). Three Federal States – Schleswig-Holstein, Berlin and\nBrandenburg – have introduced their own admission programmes. Note that the website www.resettlement.de provides more detailed statistics (under\n‘current admissions’) on every arrival that was processed through Friedland since 2015 and until the end of 2021.\nHowever, the counting differs from the Ministry of Interior, since the national and state-level humanitarian admission\n/ family reunification programmes are also included and since the statistics only refer to persons who passed through\nthe reception centre in Friedland.\nNumbers with an * come from information provided by the BAMF on 10 May 2024\n\nGermany pledged a total of 6,500 resettlement places in 2023, which is higher than in previous years.\nOut of the 6,500 places, up to 3,000 places are allocated to the national resettlement programme, up to\n200 places are foreseen for the NesT programme, up to 3,000 places are allocated for admission of Syrian\nnationals from Türkiye under the EU Türkiye statement, and 500 places are allocated to admission\nprogrammes of the Federal states of Berlin (300) and Brandenburg (200). 16 in the scheme of the aforementioned NesT programme and 21 persons under an\n“unallocated quota”.94 Through the resettlement programme with Türkiye, 2,098 Syrian nationals were\nadmitted.95 According to the BAMF, several persons admitted through the resettlement programs for 2023\nwill actually enter Germany in 2024.\n\n90\n        NeustartimTeam.de, available in German at: https://bit.ly/49l65P8.\n91\n        Ministry for the Interior, municipalities, housing and sport of Schleswig-Holstein, Erfolgreicher Abschluss des\n        Landesaufnahmeprogramms, 17 December 2021, available in German at: http://bit.ly/40zW9fB.\n92\n        BAMF, Das Bundesamt in Zahlen 2022, available in Germant at: https://bit.ly/3vVK0I6.\n93\n        Information provided by the BAMF on 10 May 2024.\n94\n        Information provided by the BAMF on 10 May 2024.\n95\n        Information provided by the BAMF on 10 May 2024.\n\n\n                                                          31", "excerpt_sha256": "ddef09a69e47e01b9f497d27576bec6f90f399363d3fdd3cbc2197a6404ce335", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0011-R03-E006", "source_id": "SRC-a007cee33f0d9205", "url": "https://asylumineurope.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/AIDA-DE_2023-Update.pdf", "title": "Country Report: Germany; For\n1,608 of those this measure was ended due to an age assessment.575\n\n      2. Special procedural guarantees\n\n                                    Indicators: Special Procedural Guarantees\n\n      1. Are there special procedural arrangements/guarantees for vulnerable people?\n                                                           Yes        For certain categories      No\n             v If for certain categories, specify which: Unaccompanied children, traumatised persons\n                                                         Victims of torture or violence\n\n         2.1. Adequate support during the interview\n\nThe BAMF does not have specialised units dealing with vulnerable groups. According to the BAMF, all\ncase workers complete the EUAA training module on ‘Interviewing Vulnerable Persons’.576 If an applicant\nor a Federal State authority submits information to the BAMF that indicates vulnerability (such as medical\nrecords or information about specific physical, mental, intellectual or sensory impairments), such\ninformation is transferred to the case worker in charge who can decide to take measures such as allocate\nmore time for the interview, appoint an interpreter of a specific gender or allow the person to bring a\ntrusted person of their choice to the interview.577\n\nFor specific groups of vulnerable persons, the BAMF employs ‘special officers’ (Sonderbeauftragte)\nresponsible for interviews and decisions on claims by applicants with special needs. Special officers also\nadvise their colleagues in dealing with vulnerable applicants and are contact persons for specialised\ncounselling services and psycho-social centres.578 Staff members who become special officers must\ncomplete a training module for the specialisation they want to achieve. In addition, they follow the EASO\ntraining modules for their specialisation.579 Training covers both the identification and in the treatment of\nvulnerable persons.580 According to the BAMF, continuous training is offered for specific topics in the realm\nof the special officers’ responsibilities.581 The BAMF guidelines stipulate that the following cases shall be\nhandled in a particularly sensitive manner and, if necessary, by specially-trained decision-makers:582\n     v Unaccompanied children;\n     v Victims of gender-specific prosecution;\n     v Victims of human trafficking; and\n     v Victims of torture and traumatised asylum seekers.\n\nAs of June 2023, a total of 1,267 BAMF had one or more roles as special officers. This corresponds to\nroughly a third of full-time equivalent positions allocated to the first instance procedure (see Number of\n\n574\n        Nerea González Méndez de Vigo, Alterseinschätzung – ein Irrgarten ohne Ausweg? The distribution among areas of responsibilities was the\nfollowing: Unaccompanied children (410), victims of gender-specific persecution (312) traumatised\npersons and victims of torture (291), victims of trafficking (254).583\n\nSpecially trained case officers may be included at all times of the asylum procedure, or take over, also\nprior to the interview, if vulnerabilitites are known. For example, if it becomes evident during the interview\nthat an asylum seeker belongs to one of these groups, the officer conducting the interview is obliged to\nconsult a special officer, in addition to notifying the reception centre if necessary and authorised by the\napplicant.584 A note on how the officers are planning to proceed must be added by the special officer to\nthe file, particularly if the special officer takes over the case as a result of their consultation. According to\ninformation provided by the government, there is an obligation in cases of unaccompanied minors for\nspecial officers to take over responsibility for the asylum procedures. In other cases of other vulnerable\ngroups, the specially trained case officer must be consulted and there are two options for further\nprocedures: either the special officer adopts an advisory role or they take over responsibility for the\nprocedure.585 However, the BAMF does not record the number of cases in which special officers are\nconsulted or in which procedures are handed over to special officers.586\n\nLawyers have reported that the introduction of special officers has led to some improvement in the\nhandling of ‘sensitive’ cases. The special officers receive special training in the area of LGBTIQ, they\nconduct interviews upon consultation by other BAMF officers and serve as multipliers for their\ncolleagues.587However, there have also been examples of cases in which indications of trauma and even\nexplicit references to torture did not lead to special officers being consulted.588 It has further been criticised\nthat there are shorcomings in the effective implementation of procedural guarantees for LGBTIQ+\npersons, which increases the risk of false decisions.589 There is no individual right to have a special officer\nhandling a person’s case, except for unaccompanied minors for whom this is mandatory. But if evidence\nsuggests that the person is vulnerable or if the person claims to have certain vulnerabilities, the interviewer\nis required to involve a special officer in the procedure, e.g. as consultant.590 However, the Administrative\nCourt of Berlin ruled that if special vulnerabilities have been detected, the absence of a special officer in\nthe asylum procedure constitutes a violation of procedural rights of vulnerable asylum seekers.591\n\n        2.2. Exemption from special procedures\n\nThe German Asylum Act exempts neither unaccompanied children nor persons with special procedural\nguarantees from the airport procedure, despite an express obligation under the recast APD to provide for\nsuch exemptions under certain conditions.592 It also makes no reference to ‘adequate support’ which\nshould be provided to those requiring special procedural guarantees.593\n\n\n\n\n583\n       The government notes that the figures cannot be added since some officers may have qualified in more than\n       one area; Federal Government, Reply to parliamentary question by The Left, 20/7503, 28 June 2023, available\n       in German at: https://bit.ly/3Sb12cF, 2-4.\n584\n       BAMF, Die Identifizierung vulnerabler Personen im Asylverfahren. For\n1,608 of those this measure was ended due to an age assessment.575\n\n      2. Special procedural guarantees\n\n                                    Indicators: Special Procedural Guarantees\n\n      1. Are there special procedural arrangements/guarantees for vulnerable people?\n                                                           Yes        For certain categories      No\n             v If for certain categories, specify which: Unaccompanied children, traumatised persons\n                                                         Victims of torture or violence\n\n         2.1. Adequate support during the interview\n\nThe BAMF does not have specialised units dealing with vulnerable groups. According to the BAMF, all\ncase workers complete the EUAA training module on ‘Interviewing Vulnerable Persons’.576 If an applicant\nor a Federal State authority submits information to the BAMF that indicates vulnerability (such as medical\nrecords or information about specific physical, mental, intellectual or sensory impairments), such\ninformation is transferred to the case worker in charge who can decide to take measures such as allocate\nmore time for the interview, appoint an interpreter of a specific gender or allow the person to bring a\ntrusted person of their choice to the interview.577\n\nFor specific groups of vulnerable persons, the BAMF employs ‘special officers’ (Sonderbeauftragte)\nresponsible for interviews and decisions on claims by applicants with special needs. Special officers also\nadvise their colleagues in dealing with vulnerable applicants and are contact persons for specialised\ncounselling services and psycho-social centres.578 Staff members who become special officers must\ncomplete a training module for the specialisation they want to achieve. In addition, they follow the EASO\ntraining modules for their specialisation.579 Training covers both the identification and in the treatment of\nvulnerable persons.580 According to the BAMF, continuous training is offered for specific topics in the realm\nof the special officers’ responsibilities.581 The BAMF guidelines stipulate that the following cases shall be\nhandled in a particularly sensitive manner and, if necessary, by specially-trained decision-makers:582\n     v Unaccompanied children;\n     v Victims of gender-specific prosecution;\n     v Victims of human trafficking; and\n     v Victims of torture and traumatised asylum seekers.\n\nAs of June 2023, a total of 1,267 BAMF had one or more roles as special officers. This corresponds to\nroughly a third of full-time equivalent positions allocated to the first instance procedure (see Number of\n\n574\n        Nerea González Méndez de Vigo, Alterseinschätzung – ein Irrgarten ohne Ausweg? The distribution among areas of responsibilities was the\nfollowing: Unaccompanied children (410), victims of gender-specific persecution (312) traumatised\npersons and victims of torture (291), victims of trafficking (254).583\n\nSpecially trained case officers may be included at all times of the asylum procedure, or take over, also\nprior to the interview, if vulnerabilitites are known. For example, if it becomes evident during the interview\nthat an asylum seeker belongs to one of these groups, the officer conducting the interview is obliged to\nconsult a special officer, in addition to notifying the reception centre if necessary and authorised by the\napplicant.584 A note on how the officers are planning to proceed must be added by the special officer to\nthe file, particularly if the special officer takes over the case as a result of their consultation. According to\ninformation provided by the government, there is an obligation in cases of unaccompanied minors for\nspecial officers to take over responsibility for the asylum procedures. In other cases of other vulnerable\ngroups, the specially trained case officer must be consulted and there are two options for further\nprocedures: either the special officer adopts an advisory role or they take over responsibility for the\nprocedure.585 However, the BAMF does not record the number of cases in which special officers are\nconsulted or in which procedures are handed over to special officers.586\n\nLawyers have reported that the introduction of special officers has led to some improvement in the\nhandling of ‘sensitive’ cases. The special officers receive special training in the area of LGBTIQ, they\nconduct interviews upon consultation by other BAMF officers and serve as multipliers for their\ncolleagues.587However, there have also been examples of cases in which indications of trauma and even\nexplicit references to torture did not lead to special officers being consulted.588 It has further been criticised\nthat there are shorcomings in the effective implementation of procedural guarantees for LGBTIQ+\npersons, which increases the risk of false decisions.589 There is no individual right to have a special officer\nhandling a person’s case, except for unaccompanied minors for whom this is mandatory. But if evidence\nsuggests that the person is vulnerable or if the person claims to have certain vulnerabilities, the interviewer\nis required to involve a special officer in the procedure, e.g. as consultant.590 However, the Administrative\nCourt of Berlin ruled that if special vulnerabilities have been detected, the absence of a special officer in\nthe asylum procedure constitutes a violation of procedural rights of vulnerable asylum seekers.591\n\n        2.2. Exemption from special procedures\n\nThe German Asylum Act exempts neither unaccompanied children nor persons with special procedural\nguarantees from the airport procedure, despite an express obligation under the recast APD to provide for\nsuch exemptions under certain conditions.592 It also makes no reference to ‘adequate support’ which\nshould be provided to those requiring special procedural guarantees.593\n\n\n\n\n583\n       The government notes that the figures cannot be added since some officers may have qualified in more than\n       one area; Federal Government, Reply to parliamentary question by The Left, 20/7503, 28 June 2023, available\n       in German at: https://bit.ly/3Sb12cF, 2-4.\n584\n       BAMF, Die Identifizierung vulnerabler Personen im Asylverfahren. This procedure is subject to youth welfare law and analogous to the taking into care of youth in\nsituations where their welfare is in acute danger. It includes the appointment of a legal guardian by the\ncompetent Family Court and the so-called ‘clearing procedure’, which includes an examination of whether\nthere are alternatives to an asylum application, such as family reunification in a third country or the\napplication for a residence permit on humanitarian grounds.621\n\nThe guardian represents the minor in all legal matters and is the first contact point for all ‘proceedings\npertaining to asylum and residence law’, including the asylum procedure.622 The legal guardian has to file\nthe asylum application for the unaccompanied minor in written form to the responsible BAMF branch\noffice.623 The guardian acts as the minor’s legal representative, but also as a personal contact person with\nwhom unaccompanied minors can develop perspectives for the future and contribute to the assistance\nplanning procedure carried out by the youth welfare office.624 While the personal interview is conducted\nwith the minor themselves, the legal guardian is present during the interview and may ask them additional\nquestions (i.e. in case the minor forgot to mention an important aspect). Subsequent applications\n\n                                 Indicators: Subsequent Applications\n      1. Does the law provide for a specific procedure for subsequent applications?                   Yes      No\n\n      2. Is a removal order suspended during the examination of a first subsequent application?\n             v At first instance:          Yes             No\n             v At the appeal stage:        Yes             No\n\n      3. Is a removal order suspended during the examination of a second, third, subsequent application?\n              v At first instance:         Yes             No\n              v At the appeal stage:       Yes             No\n\nAs of 2024, some of the information in this section will be affected by the provisions of the new Act on the\nImprovement of Removals of 18 January 2024.\n\nThe law defines a subsequent application (Folgeantrag) as any claim which is submitted after a previous\napplication has been withdrawn or has been finally rejected.632 In case of a subsequent application the\nBAMF conducts a preliminary examination on the admissibility of the application. The admissibility test is\ndetermined by the requirements for resumption of procedures as listed in the Administrative Procedure\nAct.633 According to this, a new asylum procedure is only initiated if one of the following applies:\n    v The material or legal situation on which the decision was based has subsequently changed in\n         favour of the applicant;\n    v New evidence is produced which would have resulted in a more favourable decision for the\n         applicant in the earlier procedure; or\n\n\n\n\n627\n        Federal Association for Unaccompanied Refugee Minors (BumF), Es ist 5 nach 12: Rechtsverletzungen bei\n        unbegleiteten geflüchteten Kindern und Jugendlichen, 21 November 2023, available in German at:\n        https://bit.ly/3UiVX4x.\n628\n        Uta Rieger und Nerea González Méndez de Vigo, Kindgerechte Ausgestaltung des Asylverfahrens – Eine\n        Bestandsaufnahme, in: Deutsches Kinderhilfswerk e.V. In particular, the former\nprovision could only be applied if return to the safe ‘other third country’ was possible within 3 months.\nAlthough this qualification has been removed, the provision has been applied rarely, only 24 times in 2020,\n4 times in 2021, 6 times in 2022 and 3 times in the first half of 2023 (see Admissibility procedure).692\n\n\n G. Information for asylum seekers and access to NGOs and UNHCR\n\n      1. Provision of information on the procedure\n\n                                 Indicators: Information on the Procedure\n      1. Is sufficient information provided to asylum seekers on the procedures, their rights and obligations\n         in practice?                  Yes             With difficulty         No\n\n              v Is tailored information provided to unaccompanied children?                    Yes      No\n\nAccording to Section 24(1) of the Asylum Act, the BAMF:\n\n          ‘... [S]hall inform the foreigner early on in a language he can reasonably be supposed to\n          understand about the course of the procedure and about his rights and duties, especially\n          concerning deadlines and the consequences of missing a deadline, and about possibilities to\n          return voluntarily.’\n\nThe provision was changed with the entry into force of the 2022 Act on the acceleration of asylum court\nproceedings and asylum procedures on 1 January 2023.693 The reform introduced the requirement of\ninforming applicants “early on” instead of “after the lodging of the asylum application”, which was the\nprevious wording. Information is to be provided orally in groups (see\n\nOral information). Another change introduced by the reform is the duty to inform not only about the\nasylum procedure, but also about possibilities to return voluntarily after the rejection of the asylum\napplication.\n\nFor the impact of the Covid-19 outbreak on information provision to asylum applicants see the 2021\nUpdate to the AIDA Country Report for Germany.694\n\n          1.1. Written information\n\nVarious other sections of the Asylum Act also contain obligations on the authorities to inform asylum\nseekers on certain aspects of the procedure. Oral information\n\nOral information for asylum applicants now mainly consists of the ‘voluntary independent state-run\ncounselling’ that was introduced with the so-called ‘Orderly-Return-Law’, in force since 21 August 2019\n(Section 12a Asylum Act). With the entry into force of the 2022 Act on the acceleration of asylum court\nproceedings and asylum procedures710 on 1 January 2023, the state-run counselling is to be replaced by\nindependent counselling, financed by the Federal Government but carried out by welfare associations or\n‘other civil society actors’.711 This is in line with long-standing demands form welfare associations (see\nbelow). Counselling consists of two stages: group sessions with basic information on the asylum\nprocedure as well as on return procedures, followed by the second stage of individual counselling\nsessions. The BAMF will continue to carry out the first stage of counselling as described below, whereas\nindependent organisations will carry out individual counselling.712 The funding process for independent\ncounselling associations started in February 2023 where associations could file interest for funding. After\na summary oversight, the BAMF then required the associations to file the encompassing application for\nfunding.713 EUR 20 million of financing were provided for in 2023. Welfare organisations criticise that the\nmoney was only disbursed in the summer of 2023, which delayed the availability of independent\ncounselling or caused financial gaps for those associations which provided counselling services prior to\nthe official distribution of funding. Additionally, NGOs have been criticial of the amount foreseen, stating\nthat EUR 20 million is not sufficient for nationwide independent counselling. From the AnkER centre in\nManching-Ingoldstadt, the NGO in charge can currently offer two fulltime counselling positions for up to\n600 asylum applicants, even though the BAMF’s general recommendation is one fulltime position for 180\nasylum applicants.714 The sum to be spent for personnel suffices only for early career and not for\nexperienced personnel, which make it difficult to find employees and which in combination with the high\nnumber of cases, causes an overburdening of the staff.715 Despite the envisaged goal of EUR 80 million\nannually, for 2024 again only EUR 25 million are calculated, this time for the whole year, not as in 2023\nfor the second half of the year.716 According to welfare associations, the insecurity as to how much funding\nwill be provided in the upcoming years and under which circumstances the funding will be awarded has\nled to the withdrawal of associations from their funding applications for the counselling service.717\n\n\n\n\n707\n      Memorandum Alliance, Memorandum für faire und sorgfältige Asylverfahren in Deutschland. Since there are no federal rules governing the access, it is up to the discretion of the local\nauthorities whether welfare associations have access to the centres. In Munich, the Refugee Council\ntried to provide independent mobile counselling prior 2023 and has been denied access. The Federal\nAdministrative Court upheld the denial in 2023. The court decided that access must be granted in\nindividual case after registration and only where an asylum applicant has demanded counselling.\nHowever, local authorities are not obliged to grant open access to the facilities.718 This leads to legal\nuncertainty as to whether systematic access will be provided to welfare associations under the new rules\non counselling. Overall, several associations criticise that due to the lack of funding, the uncoordinated\nfunding process and the legal uncertainty as to whether access to accommodations centres is provided,\naccess to individual counselling cannot be guaranteed in Germany.\n\nPrior to the reforms in January 2023, government advice covered the period from the lodging of the asylum\napplication to the explanation of a first instance decision; now the legal conselling can also cover appeal\nproceedings.719 According to the BAMF, the staff who offered the counselling underwent a one-week\ntraining and was ‘organisationally separated from the asylum area’.720 Procedure counselling was first\nintroduced in a pilot project together with welfare associations.721 It was then established first in all AnkER\nand functionally equivalent centres and has been rolled out to the rest of the BAMF branch offices since\n2020.722\n\nAs of 31 December 2022, counselling was available in 46 BAMF branch offices.723 Throughout 2022,\n37,644 applicants took part in the first stage counselling, while 3,147 received individual counselling\n(second stage). This is an increase in comparison to 2021 (1,928 individual sessions, while 25,784\npersons took part in group sessions), but still shows that only around 15% of the 244,132 persons who\napplied for asylum in 2022 (see Statistics) received individual advice. More information on counselling\nduring the Covid-19 outbreak can be found in the 2021 Update to the AIDA Country Report for Germany.724\nNo information on the availability of counselling and on the number of sessions is available for 2023 as of\nApril 2024.\n\nThe BAMF counselling sessions represent an improvement compared to the situation prior to August 2019\nwhen no information was systematically provided to asylum seekers.725 Nevertheless, the system is\nheavily criticised by NGOs as group counselling sessions tend to be organised within a very short period\nbefore the personal interview with the BAMF and the information provided is limited (i.e. the BAMF tends\nto provide general information on the asylum procedure, sometimes focusing only on asylum seekers’\nobligations and also on information which has nothing to do with the procedure, such as the so-called\n‘return options’).726\n\nIn addition to the counselling services as regulated by the asylum act, asylum seekers are orally informed\nabout ‘the significance and the proceedings of the interview’ and they are instructed about their rights and\n\n\n718\n      Federal Administrative Court, Decision 1 C 40.21, 28 March 2023, available in Germant at:\n      https://bit.ly/480lN0o, para. 27f.\n719\n      Section 12a (2) German Asylum Act.\n720\n      See AIDA, Country Report Germany - Update on the year 2022, April 2023, available at:\n      https://bit.ly/3UChWUr, 99.\n721\n      Information provided by the BAMF, 9 March 2023. Reference source not\nfound.Subsequent applications). The number of ‘upgrade appeal’ cases and decisions remains high,\nhowever, likely as a result of long court procedures. Between January and the end of May 2023, courts\ndecided on 5.736 such appeals, and in 806 cases (14%) granted asylum or refugee protection, while in\n4,930 cases (86%) the appeal did not lead to an improvement in the protection status.753 9,525 such\nappeals of Syrian nationals were pending as of 31 May 2023, a similarly high number to the end of 2022\n(9,458).754\n\nThe removal ban for Syria that had been in place since 2012 expired at the end of December 2020. The\nban was based on a common decision of the Federal States and the Federal government, but could not\nbe renewed due to disagreement regarding the possibility to remove criminals and ‘persons posing a risk’\nrelated to terrorist activities (‘Gefährder’).This was heavily criticised by NGOs and organisations such as\nthe German Institute for Human Rights, UNHCR and Caritas.755 The removal statistics for the first half of\n2023 indicate that 410 removals of Syrian nationals took place.756 However, Syria is not listed as a country\nof destination for removals in the first half of 2023, meaning that the removals of Syrian nationals took\nplace to other countries, for example to other EU Member States in the form of Dublin transfers or\nremovals following a refusal of entry.757 As of February 2023, the Federal Government declared that it\ncurrently sees no possibilities for removals to Syria.758\n\n      2. Afghanistan\n\nEmergency evacuation since the Taliban takeover in 2021\n\nWith the takeover of the Taliban on 15 August 2021, the German government started an evacuation\noperation for German nationals in Afghanistan as well as Afghan nationals who had worked for German\nauthorities, the military and ‘especially endangered persons’. Between 16 and 26 August 2021, a total of\n5,300 persons were evacuated, out of which 4,400 Afghan nationals. The evacuated persons entered\nGermany via an emergency visa (based on Section 14 and 22 Residence Act).759 Upon arrival, the BAMF\nthen examined whether persons had already been granted permission for an admission from abroad\n(Section 22 Residence Act). If this was not the case, and if the Federal Ministry decided no such\npermission could be granted, persons were informed of this and of the possibility to apply for asylum in\nGermany. 760\n\nAfter the end of the evacuation, German authorities continued to receive and examine notifications of risk\nof former employees and of “especially endangered individuals” in exceptional circumstances. Sub-\ncontractors and consultants who worked for German authorities only indirectly are considered on a case\nby case basis according to the Federal Government. 761 If the examination confirms that the persons are\nat risk due to their work for a German authority, admission permissions continue to be granted according\n\n753\n       Federal Government, Response to parliamentary question by The and Community.762 Permissions are also included for close family\nmembers (spouses and minor siblings), other relatives are only considered in hardship cases.763 Since\ntravelling out of Afghanistan is difficult and costly, the Federal Government has provided 32 million Euros\nin funding to the GiZ (the German Development Agency) to support persons in leaving the country. It has\nbeen criticised that despite the acknowledgement that fleeing Afghanistan is difficult, there is no\nsystematic evacuation scheme.764 As a result, there have been reports that persons who managed to\ndepart from Afghanistan have been pushed back by Bulgarian, Turkish and Greek border police.765\nPersons admitted to Germany mainly leave via Pakistan and Iran. 766 Germany has also concluded an\nagreement with the government of Pakistan to allow the concerned persons to enter Pakistan with a\nTazkira K (ID card) instead of a passport as required by the Taliban to leave the country. However, in mid-\nSeptember 2022 the Federal Government reported that a total of 34 former employees and family\nmembers are known to have died in Afghanistan.767\n\nAdmission schemes\n\nGermany has been operating an admission scheme for local staff of German ministries in Afghanistan\nsince 2013. The scheme is based on Art. 22 (2) Residence Act (Temporary residence permission to uphold\nthe political interest of the Federal Republic of Germany). The eligibility criteria depend on the status of\nthe former employee. Only former staff (and their close family members) directly employed by German\nentities are covered by the programme.768\n\nFrom the takeover of the Taliban in 2021 to 17 October 2022, according to the Federal Government,\n38,100 persons had been issued a permission for admission to Germany (out of which 24,500 were former\nemployees and eligible family members, and 13,600 were especially vulnerable persons and their eligible\nfamily members). Around 26,000 of these (68.2%) persons had entered Germany up until that time. As of\n10 December 2021, a total of 28,053 permissions for admission from abroad had been issued to Afghan\nnationals and 8,014 persons had entered Germany as of the same date.769 The admission scheme for\nlocal staff continues in parallel to the new humanitarian admission scheme announced on 17 October\n2022.\n\nOn 17 October 2022, the Federal Government launched an additional federal admissions programme\nwhich had been announced in the coalition agreement of 2021.770 The government describes the\nprogramme and procedure as follows: the programme is geared towards persons who ‘have exposed\nthemselves to particular risk through their commitment to women’s and human rights or their work in the\nspheres of justice, politics, the media, education, culture, sport or academia and are thus vulnerable’ or\n‘due to the special circumstances of their individual cases have experienced or are experiencing violence\nor persecution based on their gender, sexual orientation or gender identity or religion and are therefore at\nconcrete and personal risk. In particular, these are victims of serious individual women’s rights violations,\nhomo- or transphobic human rights violations or vulnerable representatives of religious\n\n\n\n\n762\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community, Reply to written question by Canan Bayram (The Greens),\n      20/5046, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3NvUljF, 81-121.\n763\n      Federal Government, Reply to parliamentary question by The Left, 20/3430, 15 September 2022, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/477WMjm, 11.\n764\n      Taz.de, Die Mauern werden höher, 27 February 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/48d59Ln.\n765\n      Ibid.\n766\n      Federal Government, Reply to parliamentary question by The Left, 20/3430, 15 September 2022, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/477WMjm, 2.\n767\n      Federal Government, Reply to parliamentary question by The Left, 20/3430, 15 September 2022, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/477WMjm, 16.\n768\n      ECRE, Afghans Seeking Protection in Europe, December 2021, available at: https://bit.ly/3krGfED, 11.\n769\n      Deutscher Bundestag, parliamentary question by The Left, 20/791, 22 February 2022, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3RPrRUC, 1.\n770\n      AIDA, Country Report Germany - Persons who enter Germany under the\nprogramme receive a residence permit for three years. The Federal State responsible for reception of the\npersons is to be determined according to the quota system for the distribution of asylum seekers (see\nRegistration of the asylum application), although family ties and other ‘criteria supporting integration’ are\nto be taken into account.776\n\nWhen announcing the programme, the Federal Government declared that ‘the new programme is now to\nbe implemented quickly’ and that it planned to approve around 1,000 requests per month, which is about\nthe amount of permissions granted in the months preceding the announcement. The programme is\nplanned to run until the end of the current government’s term in 2025.777 As of 30 June 2023, 229 persons\nhave been selected for admission.778 As of October 2023, only 13 of them had been admitted, due to the\npause of the admission procedure (see below).779 According to a press report, the NGOs Mission Lifeline\nand Luftbrücke Kabul alone have received around 32,000 requests as of early November 2022.780\n\nIn March 2023, the Federal Foreign office declared that all admission programs would be put to a halt for\nan indefinite time due to alleged abuse. The German newspaper Cicer and Bild published that, according\nto information they received, the admission programs had been used to bring radical Islamists to\n\n\n\n771\n      Federal Foreign Office, Joint press release by the Federal Foreign Office and the Federal Ministry of the\n      Interior and Community and Community on the federal admission programme for people from Afghanistan\n      who are at particular risk, 17 October 2022, available at: http://bit.ly/3J47ZJA.\n772\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community and Federal Foreign Office, FAQs on the humanitarian federal\n      admission programme for Afghanistan, available at: http://bit.ly/3iVp3Xx.\n773\n      rbb.de, Aufnahmeprogramm für Afghanen startet schleppend, 20 December 2022, available in German at:\n      http://bit.ly/3iVdBva.\n774\n      rbb.de, Aufnahmeprogramm für Afghanen startet schleppend, 20 December 2022, available in German at:\n      http://bit.ly/3iVdBva.\n775\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community and Federal Foreign Office, FAQs on the humanitarian federal\n      admission programme for Afghanistan, available at: http://bit.ly/3iVp3Xx.\n776\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community and Federal Foreign Office, Anordnung des\n      Bundesministeriums des Innern und für Heimat gemäß § 23 Absatz 2, Absatz 3 i. A speaker of the\nForeigner’s Office declared that there were no evidence supporting a systemic misuse of the admission\nprograms.782 Only in one case a person who applied for admission has been identified as a possible\n‘threat’ (‘Gefährder’). Nevertheless, the Foreigners Office along with the Minister of Interior decided to\nintroduce additional screening mechanisms and to halt the admission program until the screening\nmechanism is in place.783 The screening procedure involves an automatic data reconciliation with\ndatabases from the national security agencies and interviews with the German domestic intelligence\nservice (Bundesamt für Verfassungsschutz), Federal Criminal Police Office (Bundeskriminalamt), the\nFederal Police or BAMF officers acting on behalf of these national security services.784 The admission\nprograms were restarted from 26 June 2023. In the first months of the newly introduced screening\nprocedure (26 June – 21 July 2023) 99 screening interviews took place, no security concerns have been\nidentified among those.785 The Federal government further states, that the capacities for the screening\nmechanism have been expanded since its start. Accordingly, as of August 2023 several hundreds of\nscreenings can be conducted per month.786\n\nThe Left party and NGOs such as PRO ASYL welcomed the launch of the programme but criticised that\n1,000 admissions per month was too low given the ‘real pressure of persecution’ for ‘people who have\nfought for democracy and human rights’. PRO ASYL further criticised that the relatively abstract selection\ncriteria could lead to an ‘ethically highly ambivalent protection lottery’787 especially in connection with the\nfact that only authorised agencies could put forward people and that the application is through an\nalgorithm-based IT application with little room to put forward specific individual circumstances.788 The\nNGO Kabul Luftbrücke reported problems with the IT application in October and November, leading to\ndelays in sending the online forms to authorities. A further point of critique is that the programme does not\nextend to persons who have managed to flee Afghanistan.789 Several NGOs also voiced concerns over\nthe practical implementation, demanding a better staffing of the counselling and coordination centres for\nthe programme and questioning the ‘organization and content’ of the procedure,790 especially given that\nit is required to have a passport in order to leave the country while obtaining one is made extremely\ndifficult by the Taliban government. One year after the official launch, in October 2023, several NGOs\nreflected on the development of the program. They demanded that the originally envisaged number of\n1,000 admissions per months should be fulfilled, that the procedure should be more transparent and that\nin additional admission schemes are necessary to meet the needs for protection.791\n\nIn addition to the Federal Government, several Federal States (Thuringia, Berlin, Hessen and Bremen)\nhave implemented admission programmes based on family ties to Afghans living in the respective Federal\nStates (for more information see Family Reunification).
